}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{{(2{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}+18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}+{(18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}}_{\hbox to9.22pt{\vbox to9.22pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.60942pt\lower-4.60942pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.1 0 C 6.1 3.37 3.37 6.1 0 6.1 C -3.37 6.1 -6.1 3.37 -6.1 0 C -6.1 -3.37 -3.37 -6.1 0 -6.1 C 3.37 -6.1 6.1 -3.37 6.1 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

(A.41)

where
{\mathcal{C}}:=\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}
and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}:=L_{R}^{\prime}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}^{3}
, and
u(t)
and
v(t)
are parallel trannsport of
u
and
v
along
\gamma
, as defined in Lemma
29
. Some notes on notation:

1. We will use
\Gamma_{x}^{y}
and
\Gamma_{\gamma(s)}^{\gamma(t)}
to denote parallel transport along
\gamma
.

2. In subsequent parts, for
i=1,2....
, we will use

1.

\tau_{i}
to denote terms which depend super-linearly on
\delta
.

2.

\xi_{i}
, to denote terms which have
0
expectation.

3.

\theta_{i}
to denote terms which depend linearly on
\delta
, and have non-zero expectation (i.e. the important terms).

Step 2.1: Simplifying
1

By definition,
v(0)-u(0)=\delta{(\Gamma_{y}^{x}\tilde{\beta}(y)-\beta(x))}
, thus

\displaystyle\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{2\delta\bm{\left\langle}\Gamma_{y}^{x}\beta(y)-\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}}_{:=\theta_{1}}+\underbrace{2\delta\bm{\left\langle}\Gamma_{y}^{x}\tilde{\beta}(y)-\Gamma_{y}^{x}{\beta}(y),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}}_{:=\xi_{1}}

(A.42)

Step 2.2: Simplifying
2

By similar algebra as Step 2.1,

\displaystyle\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=\underbrace{\delta^{2}{\left\|\Gamma_{y}^{x}\tilde{\beta}(y)-\beta(x)\right\|}^{2}}_{:=\tau_{1}}\leq\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+\delta^{2}\sigma^{2}

(A.43)

Step 2.3: Simplifying
3

\displaystyle\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{(\gamma^{\prime}(s),(1-s)u(s)+sv(s))}(1-s)u(s)+sv(s),\gamma^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\delta\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{(\gamma^{\prime}(s),\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(s)}\zeta)}\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(s)}\zeta,\gamma^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds}_{:=\theta_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\underbrace{-\delta^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{(\gamma^{\prime}(s),\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(s)}\beta(x))}\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(s)}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds}_{:=\tau_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\underbrace{-2\delta^{3/2}\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{(\gamma^{\prime}(s),\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(s)}\zeta)}\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(s)}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds}_{:=\xi_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\underbrace{-2\delta^{2}\int_{0}^{1}s\bm{\left\langle}R{(\gamma^{\prime}(s),\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(s)}\beta(x))}\Gamma_{y}^{\gamma(s)}\tilde{\beta}(y)-\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(s)}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds}_{:=\tau_{3}}

\displaystyle\quad\underbrace{-2\delta^{3/2}\int_{0}^{1}s\bm{\left\langle}R{(\gamma^{\prime}(s),\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(s)}\zeta)}\Gamma_{y}^{\gamma(s)}\tilde{\beta}(y)-\Gamma_{x}^{\gamma(s)}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds}_{:=\xi_{3}}.

(A.44)

We will now bound
\tau_{2}
and
\tau_{3}
. By Assumption
3
,

\displaystyle{\left|\tau_{2}\right|}\leq 2\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{\beta}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}

\displaystyle{\left|\tau_{3}\right|}\leq 4\delta^{2}L_{R}{(L_{\beta}^{2}+\sigma^{2})}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}

Step 2.4: Simplifying
4

Since
4
has quite a few terms, we will bound them one by one:

\displaystyle\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}=

\displaystyle\underbrace{2{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}}_{:=\tau_{5}}+\underbrace{18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}}_{:=\tau_{6}}+\underbrace{18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}}_{:=\tau_{7}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{4{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}}_{:=\tau_{8}}+\underbrace{4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}}_{:=\tau_{9}}.

Recall that
{\mathcal{C}}:=\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}
and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}:=L_{R}^{\prime}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}^{3}
. Following previous calculations, we can bound
{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}\leq\delta\sigma+\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}
. We can also bound
{\left\|u\right\|}\leq\delta L_{\beta}+\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\zeta\right\|}
and
{\left\|v\right\|}\leq\delta L_{\beta}+\delta\sigma+\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\zeta\right\|}
. Thus

\displaystyle\tau_{5}=

\displaystyle 2{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{\mathcal{C}}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4L_{R}{({\left\|u\right\|}^{2}+{\left\|v\right\|}^{2})}e^{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16L_{R}{(\delta^{2}{(2L_{\beta}^{2}+\sigma^{2})}+\delta{\left\|\zeta\right\|}^{2})}\cdot\exp{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{(\delta{(2L_{\beta}+\sigma)}+2\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\zeta\right\|})})}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot{(\delta L_{\beta}+\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\zeta\right\|})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 128L_{R}e^{2\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\zeta\right\|}}\cdot{(\delta^{2}{\left\|\zeta\right\|}^{4}+\delta^{4}{(L_{\beta}^{4}+\sigma^{4})})},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}\left[{\tau_{5}^{4}}\right]}^{1/4}\leq

\displaystyle 512L_{R}\cdot{(\delta d+\delta^{2}{(L_{\beta}^{2}+\sigma^{2})})}{(\delta^{2}\sigma^{2}+\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2})},

where for the third line, we use the fact that our bound on
\delta
implies that
\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{32\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta}},\frac{1}{32\sqrt{L_{R}}\sigma}\right\}
.

By similar algebra, we verify that

\displaystyle\tau_{6}

\displaystyle=18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2048L_{R}^{2}e^{4\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\zeta\right\|}}\cdot{(\delta^{2}{\left\|\zeta\right\|}^{4}+\delta^{4}{(L_{\beta}^{4}+\sigma^{4})})}{(\delta^{2}\sigma^{2}+\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2})},

\displaystyle\tau_{7}

\displaystyle=18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2048L_{R}^{2}e^{4\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\zeta\right\|}}\cdot{(\delta^{2}{\left\|\zeta\right\|}^{4}+\delta^{4}{(L_{\beta}^{4}+\sigma^{4})})}\cdot\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2},

\displaystyle\tau_{8}

\displaystyle=4{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 128L_{R}^{\prime}e^{4\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\zeta\right\|}}\cdot{(\delta^{3/2}{\left\|\zeta\right\|}^{3}+\delta^{3}{(L_{\beta}^{3}+\sigma^{3})})}\cdot\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2},

\displaystyle\tau_{9}

\displaystyle=4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq 128L_{R}e^{4\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\zeta\right\|}}\cdot{(\delta{\left\|\zeta\right\|}^{2}+\delta^{2}{(L_{\beta}^{2}+\sigma^{2})})}\cdot{(\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+\delta\sigma\mathrm{d}{(x,y)})},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}\left[{\tau_{6}^{4}}\right]}^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{14}L_{R}^{2}\cdot{(\delta^{2}d^{2}+\delta^{4}{(L_{\beta}^{4}+\sigma^{4})})}{(\delta^{2}\sigma^{2}+\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2})},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}\left[{\tau_{7}^{4}}\right]}^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{14}L_{R}^{2}\cdot{(\delta^{2}d^{2}+\delta^{4}{(L_{\beta}^{4}+\sigma^{4})})}\cdot\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}\left[{\tau_{8}^{4}}\right]}^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq 512L_{R}^{\prime}\cdot{(\delta^{3/2}d^{3/2}+\delta^{3}{(L_{\beta}^{3}+\sigma^{3})})}\cdot\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2},

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}\left[{\tau_{9}^{4}}\right]}^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq 512L_{R}\cdot{(\delta d+\delta^{2}{(L_{\beta}^{2}+\sigma^{2})})}\cdot{(\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+\delta\sigma\mathrm{d}{(x,y)})},

(A.45)

where we use Lemma
21
and the fact that our assumption on
\delta
implies that
\delta\leq\frac{1}{2^{14}L_{R}d}
.

Step 3: Putting Things Together

Let
\mathbb{E}\left[{\cdot}\right]
denote expectation wrt the
\zeta
and
\tilde{\beta}
(which is a random function). By Assumption
2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\theta_{1}}\right]\leq-2\delta m\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}.

By definition of Ricci curvature and by Assumption
1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\theta_{2}}\right]\leq\delta L_{Ric}.

Using our assumed bound on
\delta
, we verify that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}+\tau_{3}+\tau_{5}+\tau_{6}+\tau_{7}+\tau_{8}+\tau_{9}}\right]\leq\frac{\delta(m-L_{Ric}/2)}{2}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+16\delta^{2}\sigma^{2}.

We omit the proof for this fact, since it follows by basic but long algebra, but note that we need to apply Young’s inequality at several points. We also verify that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\xi_{1}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\xi_{2}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\xi_{3}}\right]=0.

Plugging everything into (
A.41
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})}^{2}}\right]\leq{(1-\delta{(m-L_{Ric}/2)})}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+16\delta^{2}\sigma^{2}.

∎

Appendix B
Distance Contraction under Kendall Cranston Coupling

In this section, we prove Lemma
3
, which is the main tool for proving mixing of manifold diffusion processes under the distant dissipativity assumption. We note again that the proof is entirely based on existing results from
[
Eberle 2016
,
Hsu 2002
]
, and is only included for completeness.

B.1
The Kendall Cranston Coupling

Lemma 9
.

Let
T\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be some fixed time. Assume that there is are constants
L_{\beta},L_{\beta}^{\prime}
such that for all
x,y\in M
,
{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\beta}
and
{\left\|\beta(x)-\Gamma_{y}^{x}\beta(y)\right\|}\leq L_{\beta}^{\prime}{\left\|x-y\right\|}
. Let
i
be some integer satisfying

i\geq\max\left\{\log_{2}{(32T\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta})},\log_{2}{(32Td)},\log_{2}{(32L_{\beta}T)}\right\}
.

For any
x,y
, let
\Lambda(x,y)
denote the set of minimizing geodesics from
x
to
y
, i.e. for any
\gamma\in\Lambda(x,y)
,
\gamma(0)=x
,
\gamma(1)=y
,
\forall t,\nabla_{\gamma^{\prime}(t)}\gamma^{\prime}(t)=0
and
\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}={\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}
. Let
\kappa(r):=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\sup_{\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}=r}\inf_{\gamma\in\Lambda(x,y)}\bm{\left\langle}\Gamma_{y}^{x}\beta(y)-\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}
.

Let
x,y\in M
and let
E^{x}
be an arbitrary orthonormal basis of
T_{x}M
and let
E^{y}
be an arbitrary orthonormal basis of
T_{y}
. let
x^{i}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;x,E^{x},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{x},i)
and
y^{i}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;y,E^{y},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{y},i)
where
\mathbf{B}^{x}
and
\mathbf{B}^{y}
are standard Brownian motion in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and where
\overline{\Phi}
is as defined in (
A.3
).

For any
\varepsilon
, there exists a coupling between
\mathbf{B}^{x}
and
\mathbf{B}^{y}
, and Brownian motion
\mathbf{W}^{i}
over
\mathbb{R}
, such that for all
k\in\left\{0...2^{i}\right\}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\delta^{i}{(2\kappa{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})})}+L_{Ric})})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}{(4\delta^{i}-4\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}{(\mathbf{W}^{i}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{W}^{i}{(k\delta^{i})})})}

\displaystyle\quad+\tau^{i}_{k}

where
\tau^{i}_{k}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{{\left|\tau^{i}_{k}\right|}}\right]\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{1}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2}{(1+L_{\beta}^{4})}{(1+\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2})}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{{{\tau}^{i}_{k}}^{2}}\right]\leq{\mathcal{C}}_{1}{{(1+\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{4})}{\delta^{i}}^{2}}

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
is a constant depending on
L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},d,T
.

Proof.

We set up some notation: throughout this proof, consider a fixed
i
. Recall that
\delta^{i}:=T/2^{i}
, and assume
i
is large enough such that
\delta^{i}\leq\frac{1}{32\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{\beta}}
. Let
x^{i}_{k}
be as defined in (
4.1
) so that
x^{i}_{k}=x^{i}(k\delta^{i})
. Let us also define
K:=2^{i}
, so that
T=K\delta^{i}
.

Step 1: defining the coupling

By definition, for any
k\in\left\{0...K\right\}
,

\displaystyle x^{i}_{k+1}:=\Exp_{x^{i}_{k}}{(\delta^{i}\beta{(x^{i}_{k})}+{{(\mathbf{B}^{x}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}^{x}{(k\delta^{i})})}}\circ E^{i}_{k})}

\displaystyle y^{i}_{k+1}:=\Exp_{y^{i}_{k}}{(\delta^{i}\beta{(y^{i}_{k})}+{{(\mathbf{B}^{y}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}^{y}{(k\delta^{i})})}}\circ\tilde{E}^{i}_{k})}

Let
\gamma^{i}_{k}:[0,1]\to M
denote a minimizing geodesic from
x^{i}_{k}
to
y^{i}_{k}
.

Let
F^{i}_{k}
be an orthonormal basis at
T_{y^{i}_{k}}M
, obtained from the parallel transport of
E^{i}_{k}
along
\gamma^{i}_{k}
, i.e. for all
j=1...d
,

\displaystyle F^{i,j}_{k}=\Gamma_{\gamma^{i}_{k}}E^{i,j}_{k}

Let us define
\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
as matrix whose
a,b
entry is

\displaystyle{\left[\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}\right]}_{a,b}=\bm{\left\langle}F^{i,a}_{k},\tilde{E}^{i,b}_{k}\bm{}

one can verify that
\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}
is an orthogonal matrix, and that for all
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbf{v}\circ F^{i}_{k}=\mathbf{M}\mathbf{v}\circ\tilde{E}^{i}_{k}
.

Let us define
\bar{\bm{\nu}}^{i}_{k}
denote the unique coordinates of
\frac{{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(1)}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(1)\right\|}}
wrt
F^{i}_{k}
(equivalently the coordinates of
\frac{{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0)}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}}
wrt
E^{i}_{k}
). We define
\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}:={\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}\bar{\bm{\nu}}^{i}_{k}
.

We now define a coupling between
\mathbf{B}^{x}(t)
and
\mathbf{B}^{y}(t)
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}^{y}(t):=\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{t\in[k\delta^{i},(k+1)\delta^{i})\right\}}\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}d\mathbf{B}^{x}(t)

For this to be a valid coupling, it suffices to verify that

\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{t\in[k\delta^{i},(k+1)\delta^{i})\right\}}\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}d\mathbf{B}^{x}(t)
is indeed a standard Brownian motion. This can be done by verifying that the definition satisfies Levy’s characterization of Brownian motion. We omit the proof, but highlight two important facts: 1.
\int_{0}^{T}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{t\in[k\delta^{i},(k+1)\delta^{i})\right\}}\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}
is adapted to the natural filtration of
\mathbf{B}^{x}(t)
, and 2.
\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}
is an orthogonal matrix. We have thus defined a coupling between
\mathbf{B}^{x}
and
\mathbf{B}^{y}
, and consequently, a coupling between
x^{i}(t)
and
y^{i}(t)
for all
t
.

Step 2: Applying Lemma
29

Having defined a coupling between
x^{i}_{k}
and
y^{i}_{k}
, we bound
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{K},y^{i}_{K})}^{2}}\right]
for
K:=T/\delta^{i}=2^{i}
by applying Lemma
29
, with
x=x^{i}_{k}
,
y=y^{i}_{k}
,
u=\delta^{i}\beta{(x^{i}_{k})}+{{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}}\circ E^{i}_{k}
,
v=\delta^{i}\beta{(y^{i}_{k})}+{{(\tilde{\mathbf{B}}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\tilde{\mathbf{B}}{(k\delta^{i})})}}\circ\tilde{E}^{i}_{k}
and
\gamma:=\gamma^{i}_{k}
.

Following the notation in Lemma
29
, let
u(t)
and
v(t)
be the parallel transport of
u
and
v
along
\gamma(t)
. We verify that
u(s)=\delta^{i}\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(x^{i}_{k})+{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k}
and that

\displaystyle v(s)=

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})+\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\tilde{E}^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})+{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}F^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})+{(I-2\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}{\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}}^{T})}{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})+{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad-2\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}\frac{{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}}

where the second equality is by definition of
\mathbf{M}^{i}_{k}
, the third equality is by definition of
F^{i}_{k}
, the fourth equality is by definition of
\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}
and the fact that
\gamma^{i}_{k}
is a geodesic. It is convenient subsequently to note the following:

\displaystyle v(s)-u(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta^{i}{(\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})-\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(x^{i}_{k}))}-2\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}\frac{{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}}

and

\displaystyle(1-s)u(s)+sv(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-s)\delta^{i}\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(x^{i}_{k})+s\delta^{i}\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}\beta(y^{i}_{k})

\displaystyle\quad+{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle\quad-2s\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}\frac{{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}}

Step 3: Reorganizing Lemma
29

With
u,v
as defined above, Lemma
29
implies that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}+{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),(1-s)u(s)+sv(s))}(1-s)u(s)+sv(s),{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+{(2{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}+18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}+{(18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

(B.1)

where
{\mathcal{C}}:=\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}
and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}:=L_{R}^{\prime}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}^{3}
.

Below, we bound each of the terms above

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}

\displaystyle=2\delta^{i}\bm{\left\langle}{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0),\Gamma_{y^{i}_{k}}^{x^{i}_{k}}\beta(y^{i}_{k})-\beta(x^{i}_{k})\bm{}-4{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(0)\right\|}\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}

\displaystyle{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{{\delta^{i}}^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+4\delta^{i}L_{\beta}{\left|\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}\right|}}_{\tau^{i}_{k,1}}

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),(1-s)u(s)+sv(s))}(1-s)u(s)+sv(s),{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k})}{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k},{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{{\delta^{i}}^{2}L_{R}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}L_{\beta}^{2}+4\delta^{i}L_{R}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}L_{\beta}{\left\|\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\right\|}_{2}}_{\tau^{i}_{k,2}}

In the first equality above, we crucially use the fact that
\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}\frac{{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)}{{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}}
is a scalar multiple of
\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(s)
, and the fact that
\bm{\left\langle}R(u,u)v,u\bm{}=\bm{\left\langle}R(u,v)u,u\bm{}=0
for all
u,v
by symmetry of the Riemannian curvature tensor.

Finally, we will take the remaining terms, and denote them by

\displaystyle\tau^{i}_{k,3}:=

\displaystyle{(2{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}+18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{(18{\mathcal{C}}^{4}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}+4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{2{\mathcal{C}}}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

We claim that under our assumption on
i
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{{\left|\tau^{i}_{k,1}+\tau^{i}_{k,2}+\tau^{i}_{k,3}\right|}}\right]=O{({\delta^{i}}^{3/2}{(1+L_{\beta}^{4})}{(1+\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2})})}

where
O()
hides dependencies on
L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},d,T
.

We omit the proof for the above claim, which involves some tedious but straightforward algebra, but we note that the proof uses
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\right\|}_{2}^{j}}\right]=O{({\delta^{i}}^{j/2})}
(for all integer
j
) and that
\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(a{\left\|\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\right\|}_{2})}}\right]\leq 4\exp{(2a^{2}\delta^{i}d)}\leq 8
for
\delta^{i}a^{2}\leq 1/32
(see Lemma
21
). It is also important to use our assumption on
\delta^{i}
in the lemma statement.

We simplify (
B.1
) to

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta^{i}\kappa{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}-4\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+4\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k})}{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k},{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\tau^{i}_{k,1}+\tau^{i}_{k,2}+\tau^{i}_{k,3}

(B.2)

Step 4: Pulling out the expectation

We will further simplify (
B.2
) by replacing a few terms by their expectations. Define

\displaystyle{\tau}^{i}_{k,4}:=\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k})},{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k},{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}

\displaystyle\qquad-\delta^{i}Ric{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s))}ds

\displaystyle{\tau}^{i}_{k,5}:=\delta^{i}-\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}^{2}

By definition of Ricci Curvature and by Assumption
1
,

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R{({\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s),{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k})},{(\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})})}\circ\Gamma_{x^{i}_{k}}^{\gamma^{i}_{k}(s)}E^{i}_{k},{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\delta^{i}L_{Ric}{\left\|{\gamma^{i}_{k}}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}^{2}=\leq-\delta^{i}L_{Ric}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

where
Ric
denotes the Ricci curvature tensor.

By definition of
\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}^{2}}\right]=\delta^{i}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}
.

Let
\tau^{i}_{k}:=\tau^{i}_{k,1}+\tau^{i}_{k,2}+\tau^{i}_{k,3}
.
We can thus further simplify (
B.2
) to

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta^{i}\kappa{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}-4\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+4\delta^{i}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}-\delta^{i}L_{Ric}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\tau^{i}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta^{i}{(2\kappa{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})})}+L_{Ric})}\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-4\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}\bm{\left\langle}\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}+4\delta^{i}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}

\displaystyle\quad+\tau^{i}_{k}

(B.3)

the conclusion follows by defining
\mathbf{W}^{i}(t):=\int_{0}^{t}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{t\in[k\delta^{i},(k+1)\delta^{i}]\right\}}\bm{\left\langle}\bar{\bm{\nu}}^{i}_{k},\mathbf{B}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{B}{(k\delta^{i})}\bm{}
and verifying that it is a Brownian motion. (Recall our definition that
\bm{\nu}^{i}_{k}:={\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}>\varepsilon\right\}}\bar{\bm{\nu}}^{i}_{k}
)
∎

B.2
Lyapunov function and its smooth approximation

In this section, we consider a Lyapunov function
f
taken from
Eberle 2016
. By analyzing how
f(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})})
evolves under the dynamic in Lemma
9
, one can demonstrate that the distance function contracts.

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. We will see later that
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
will correspond to distant-dissipativity parameters in (
2
).

Let
\varepsilon\in[0,\infty)
. One should think of
\varepsilon
as being arbitrarily small, as eventually we are only interested in the limit as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Define functions
\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)
,
\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)
and
\nu(r)
, all from
\mathbb{R}^{+}
to
\mathbb{R}
:

\displaystyle\mu_{\varepsilon}(r)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\text{for }r\leq\mathcal{R}\\
1-(r-\mathcal{R})/{(\varepsilon)},&\text{for }r\in{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon}\\
0,&\text{for }r\geq\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\nu_{\varepsilon}(r):=1-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds}{\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds}

\displaystyle\psi_{\varepsilon}(r):=e^{-\frac{\mathcal{L}\int_{0}^{r}r\mu_{\varepsilon}(r)dr}{2}}

\displaystyle\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r):=\int_{0}^{r}\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)ds,

We defined an
\varepsilon
-smoothed Lyapunov function as

Definition 1
.

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}(r):=\int_{0}^{r}\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)\nu_{\varepsilon}(s)ds

\displaystyle g_{\varepsilon}(s)=f_{\varepsilon}{(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})}

The case when
\varepsilon=0
(when there is no smoothing) will be of particular interest to us:

Definition 2
.

\displaystyle f(r):=f_{0}(r)=g_{0}(r)

Remark 2
.

The Lyapunov function from
Eberle 2016
is more general, but for the specific case of
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{R}
distant dissipative functions, it is equal to
f
as defined in (
2
).

Lemma 10
.

Assume
\varepsilon\in[0,1/(4\sqrt{\mathcal{L}})]
, then
f_{\varepsilon}
as defined in (
1
) satisfies

\displaystyle 1.\ f_{\varepsilon}(r)\in[\frac{1}{2}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}r,r]\qquad

for all
r

\displaystyle 2.\ f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)\in[\frac{1}{2}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)},1]\qquad

for all
r

\displaystyle 3.\ f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)\in[-4{\mathcal{L}}^{3/2},0]\qquad

for all
r

\displaystyle 4.\ f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\mathcal{L}rf_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)\leq-\frac{\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}f_{\varepsilon}(r)\qquad

for
r\in[0,\mathcal{R}]

If in addition,
\varepsilon>0
,
f_{\varepsilon}
satisfies

\displaystyle 5.\ {\left|f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)\right|}\leq\frac{256\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon}\qquad\text{for all $r$}

Proof.

We can verify that

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=\psi_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)+\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{L}\mu_{\varepsilon}(r)r\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)+\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{L}\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)+\mathcal{L}r\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)+\mathcal{L}^{2}r^{2}\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)-2\mathcal{L}r\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)+\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)

1. follows from integrating 2.

2. follows from
\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)\in[1/2,1]
and
\psi_{\varepsilon}\in[\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)},1]
and the expression for
f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)
above.

3. follows from
\mu_{\varepsilon},\psi_{\varepsilon},\nu_{\varepsilon}\geq 0
and
\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\leq 0
, and the fact that
r\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\leq 2\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}
and (
B.4
).

4. is a little more involved. First note that over
r\in[0,\mathcal{R}]
,
\mu_{\varepsilon}(r)=1
. This will simplify some calculations. From the expression for
f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}
above, we verify

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\mathcal{L}rf_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)=\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)=-\frac{\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)}{2\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds}

We can bound the denominator as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds\leq\int_{0}^{\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon}\frac{\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds\leq\frac{\int_{0}^{\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)ds}{\psi{(\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon)}}\leq\frac{(1+\varepsilon)^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{2\exp{(-\mathcal{L}(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}

where the first inequality is by
\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\leq 1
, and
\mu_{\varepsilon}(r)=0
for
r\geq\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon
the second inequality is by
\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)
being monotonically decreasing, and the third inequality is by
\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\leq r
.Finally, note that
\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\geq f_{\varepsilon}(r)
. Put together,

\displaystyle f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\mathcal{L}rf_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(r)\leq-\frac{\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}f_{\varepsilon}(r)

We now prove the bound for 5. It is useful to recall that
\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\leq 1
and
\nu_{\varepsilon}(r)\leq 1
.

\displaystyle\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)=\int_{0}^{r}\exp{(-\mathcal{L}s^{2})}ds\leq\frac{4}{\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds\geq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\mathcal{R}}\frac{\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds\geq\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1/\sqrt{2\mathcal{L}}}\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)ds\geq\frac{1}{16\mathcal{L}}

(B.4)

\displaystyle{\left|\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}(r)\right|}\leq\frac{\Psi{(\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon)}}{2\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds}\leq 8\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}

For
r\in[0,\mathcal{R}+\varepsilon]
(
\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}=0
outside this range),

\displaystyle{\left|\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\nu_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)\right|}\leq\frac{\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Psi{(r)}+r\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)/\varepsilon+\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)+2r\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)/\psi_{\varepsilon}(r)}{2\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mu_{\varepsilon}(s)\Psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}{\psi_{\varepsilon}(s)}ds}\leq 32\mathcal{L}\cdot\Psi_{\varepsilon}(r)\cdot{(\frac{2}{\varepsilon}+2\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R})}\leq\frac{128\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon}

We can thus bound
{\left|f^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)\right|}
as

\displaystyle{\left|f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(r)\right|}\leq 2\mathcal{L}+16\mathcal{L}^{3/2}\mathcal{R}+\frac{128\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon}\leq\frac{256\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon}

∎

Lemma 11
.

Assume
\varepsilon\in(0,1/(4\sqrt{\mathcal{L}})]

\displaystyle 1.\ {g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(s)}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{s+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})

\displaystyle 2.\ {g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(s)}=\frac{1}{4{(s+\varepsilon)}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{4{(s+\varepsilon)}^{3/2}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})

\displaystyle 3.\ {g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)}=\frac{1}{8{(s+\varepsilon)}^{3/2}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})-\frac{1}{8{(s+\varepsilon)}^{2}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})+\frac{1}{6{(s+\varepsilon)}^{5/2}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(\sqrt{s+\varepsilon})

\displaystyle 4.\ {\left|g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)\right|}\leq O{(\varepsilon^{-5/2})}\qquad\text{for all $s$}

where
O()
notation hides dependency on
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
.

Proof.

The first 3 points follow from chain rule.

The last point follows from point 5 from Lemma
10
.

\displaystyle{\left|g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)\right|}\leq\frac{64\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon^{5/2}\mathcal{R}}+\frac{\sqrt{\mathcal{L}}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{5/2}}

∎

B.3
Contraction of Lyapunov Function under Kendall Cranston Coupling

Lemma 12
.

Consider the same setup as Lemma
9
. For any
x,y
, let
\Lambda(x,y)
denote the set of minimizing geodesics from
x
to
y
, i.e. for any
\gamma\in\Lambda(x,y)
,
\gamma(0)=x
,
\gamma(1)=y
,
\forall t,\nabla_{\gamma^{\prime}(t)}\gamma^{\prime}(t)=0
and
\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}={\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}
. Let
\kappa(r):=\frac{1}{r^{2}}\sup_{\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}=r}\inf_{\gamma\in\Lambda(x,y)}\bm{\left\langle}\Gamma_{y}^{x}\beta(y)-\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}
.

Assume there exists
\mathcal{R}\geq 0,q\leq 0
such that
\kappa(r)\leq q
for all
r\leq\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{L}=q+L_{Ric}/2
. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1/(4\sqrt{\mathcal{L}})]
. Let
g_{\varepsilon}
be as defined in
1
with parameters
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
. Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{k}
denote the natural filtration generated by
x^{i}_{k}
and
y^{i}_{k}
.

There exists a constant
c_{1}
, depending on
L_{\beta},L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{R},T,d
, and some constant
c_{2}
, depending on
L_{\beta}^{\prime},L_{Ric},\mathcal{R}
such that for any
i>c_{1}
and
\varepsilon>c_{2}
, there exists a coupling between
x^{i}_{k}
and
y^{i}_{k}
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r>\mathcal{R}\right\}}\delta^{i}{({(\kappa(r_{k})+L_{Ric}/2)}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}/8)}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{2{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r\leq\mathcal{R}\right\}}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2})}}\right]+O{(\delta^{i}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}

where
O{()}
hides dependency on
L_{R},L_{\beta}^{\prime},T,d
.

Proof.

Let us define, for convenience,
r_{k}:=\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}
. By Lemma
9
, for any
i
and any
\varepsilon
, there exists a coupling satisfying

\displaystyle r_{k+1}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\delta^{i}{(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+L_{Ric})})}r_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}{(4\delta^{i}-4r_{k}\mathbf{W}^{i}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{W}^{i}{(k\delta^{i})})}+\tau^{i}_{k}

where
\tau^{i}_{k}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left|\tau^{i}_{k}\right|}}\right]\leq O{({\delta^{i}}^{3/2}{(1+L_{\beta}^{4})}{(1+r_{k}^{2})})}\qquad\mathbb{E}\left[{{{\tau}^{i}_{k}}^{2}}\right]\leq O{({{(1+r_{k}^{4})}{\delta^{i}}^{2}})}

where
O{()}
hides dependencies on
L_{R},L_{R}^{\prime},d,T
.

By third order Taylor expansion,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot{(\delta^{i}{(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+L_{Ric})})}r_{k}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot 4\delta^{i}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{1}{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot{(4r_{k}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}{(\mathbf{W}^{i}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{W}^{i}{(k\delta^{i})})})}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+O{(\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}

(B.5)

The last line uses two facts:

1.

From Lemma
13
, for any
j
, there exists a constant
{\mathcal{C}}
, depending on
T,d,L_{R},L_{\beta}^{\prime}
, but independent of
L_{\beta}
, such that for all
i,k
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},x_{0})}^{2j}}\right]<{\mathcal{C}}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(y^{i}_{k},y_{0})}^{2j}}\right]<{\mathcal{C}}
.

2.

Roughly speaking,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}^{2}}\right]=O{({\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}
. More specifically:

\displaystyle{\left|\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k+1},y^{i}_{k+1})}-\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},y^{i}_{k})}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k}x^{i}_{k+1})}+2\mathrm{d}{(y^{i}_{k}y^{i}_{k+1})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\delta^{i}{({\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}+{\left\|\beta(y_{0})\right\|}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},x_{0})})}+L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{k},x_{0})})})}

\displaystyle\quad+4{\left\|\mathbf{B}((k+1)\delta^{i})-\mathbf{B}(k\delta^{i})\right\|}_{2}

Plugging in the definition of
g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}
and
g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}
,

\displaystyle g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot{(\delta^{i}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+L_{Ric})})}r_{k}^{2}+4{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\delta^{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2}+4{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}+2{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{\delta^{i}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}}{\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}

where we use the assumption that
\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{4\mathcal{R}^{2}}
and
\varepsilon<1
.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{\frac{1}{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot{(4r_{k}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}{(\mathbf{W}^{i}{((k+1)\delta^{i})}-\mathbf{W}^{i}{(k\delta^{i})})})}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}g_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(r_{k}^{2})}\cdot{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}}{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}-{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}}{{(r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon)}^{3/2}}

Note that
r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}
implies that
\frac{r_{k}^{2}}{{(r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon)}^{3/2}}\geq\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon^{1/3}}
. Thus

\displaystyle 2{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{\delta^{i}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}}{\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}-{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}}{{(r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon)}^{3/2}}\leq 4\delta^{i}\varepsilon^{1/3}

(B.6)

where we use the fact that
{\left|f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\right|}\leq 1
.

We now bound
\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r_{k}>\varepsilon^{1/3}\right\}}\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}}{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}
. Consider three cases:

1.

r_{k}\leq\varepsilon^{1/3}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}\leq{\delta^{i}{(q+L_{Ric}/2)}}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}

2.

r_{k}\in(\varepsilon^{1/3},\mathcal{R}]
:

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}+\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}}{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}}{{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}{(\mathcal{L}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}+2f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{2{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}\delta^{i}f_{\varepsilon}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}

where we use Lemma
10
and the definition of
\mathcal{L}
.

3.

r_{k}>\mathcal{R}
: We use the fact that
f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}(r)\leq 0
for all
r\geq\mathcal{R}\geq\varepsilon
. Thus

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}+\frac{2\delta^{i}r_{k}^{2}}{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{i}}{2\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}{({(2\kappa{(r_{k})}+L_{Ric})}r_{k}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta^{i}{({(\kappa(r_{k})+L_{Ric}/2)})}r_{k}^{2}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}}{8\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\delta^{i}{({(\kappa(r_{k})+L_{Ric}/2)}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)})}f_{\varepsilon}{(\sqrt{r_{k}^{2}+\varepsilon})}

∎

Proof of Lemma
3
.

Let
E^{x}
be an orthonormal basis of
T_{x(0)}M
,
E^{y}
be an orthonormal basis of
T_{y(0)}M
, and let
\mathbf{B}^{x}
and
\mathbf{B}^{y}
denote two Brownian motions which may be coupled in a non-trivial way. By definition of
\Phi
in (
A.4
) and by Lemma
2
,
x(t)=\Phi(t;x(0),E^{x},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{x})
and
y(t)=\Phi(t;y(0),E^{y},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{y})
, where equivalence is in the sense of distribution.

Lemma
12
almost gives us what we need. However, because we assumed that
\beta
satisfies Assumption
2
, the assumption that
{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}\leq L_{\beta}
cannot possibly hold. We thus need to approximate
\beta
by a sequence of increasingly non-Lipschitz functions.

Consider a fixed
i
. Let
s^{j}
be a sequence of increasing radius, such that
s^{j}\to\infty
as
j\to\infty
. Let
\beta^{j}
denote the truncation of
\beta
to norm
s^{j}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\beta^{j}(x):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\beta(x)&\text{for }{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}\leq s^{j}\\
\beta(x)\cdot\frac{s^{j}}{{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}}&\text{for }{\left\|\beta(x)\right\|}>s^{j}\end{array}\right..

We verify that
\beta^{j}
also satisfies Assumption
3
with the same
L_{\beta}^{\prime}
as
\beta
.

Consider some fixed
j
. Let us now define the Euler Murayama discretization of
x(t)
and
y(t)
as

\displaystyle x^{i}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;x(0),E^{x},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{x},i)

\displaystyle y^{i}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;y(0),E^{y},\beta,\mathbf{B}^{y},i).

Where
\overline{\Phi}
is as defined in (
A.3
), and is a short-hand for the (interpolated) Euler Murayama sequence with stepsize
\delta^{i}=T/2^{i}
, defined in (
4.2
) (equivalently (
A.2
)). It is by definition that
x(t)=\lim_{i\to\infty}x^{i}(t)
(and similarly for
y(t)
and
y^{i}(t)
).

Furthermore, define, for all
i,j
,

\displaystyle\tilde{x}^{i,j}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;x(0),E^{x},\beta^{j},\mathbf{B}^{x},i),

\displaystyle\tilde{y}^{i,j}(t):=\overline{\Phi}(t;y(0),E^{y},\beta^{j},\mathbf{B}^{y},i),

\displaystyle\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(t):={\Phi}(t;x(0),E^{x},\beta^{j},\mathbf{B}^{x}),

\displaystyle\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(t):={\Phi}(t;y(0),E^{y},\beta^{j},\mathbf{B}^{y}).

Note that the above definition implies a non-trivial coupling between
\tilde{x}^{i,j}(t)
and
x^{i}(t)
, via the shared Brownian motion
\mathbf{B}^{x}
. In words,
\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(t)
denotes the exact Langevin SDE, but with drift given by
\beta^{j}
(the truncated version of
\beta
), and
\tilde{x}^{i,j}
denotes the (interpolated) Euler Murayama discretization of
\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}
with stepsize
\delta^{i}
.

Let
L_{0}:=\max\left\{{\left\|\beta(x(0))\right\|},{\left\|\beta(y(0))\right\|}\right\}
. Let us define
{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}:=\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}_{k},\tilde{y}^{i,j}_{k})}
. Let
\kappa(r)
be as defined in the statement of Lemma
12
.

Using Assumption
2
, we verify that

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}>\mathcal{R}\right\}}{(\kappa({\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})+L_{Ric}/2)}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathcal{R}<{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\leq\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\right\}}{(-m+L_{Ric}/2)}+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathcal{R}<{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}},\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}}{(\frac{s^{j}}{{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}}+L_{Ric}/2)}.

Let
q,\mathcal{R}
be the parameters in Assumption
2
. This implies that
\kappa(r)\leq q
for all
r\leq\mathcal{R}
. Let
\mathcal{L}:=q+L_{Ric}/2
and
\varepsilon
be as defined in Lemma
12
. Let
g_{\varepsilon}
be as defined in Definition
1
with parameters
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{R}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r>\mathcal{R}\right\}}\delta^{i}{({(\kappa({\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})+L_{Ric}/2)}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}/8)}g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{2{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r\leq\mathcal{R}\right\}}g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]+O{(\delta^{i}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta^{i}(m-L_{Ric}/2)\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{16}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathcal{R}<{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\leq\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\right\}}g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\delta^{i}\exp{(-(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2}/2)}}{2{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r\leq\mathcal{R}\right\}}g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}<{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}}{({s^{j}}+\frac{1}{2}L_{Ric}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})}}\right]+O{(\delta^{i}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\alpha_{\varepsilon}\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{k+1}^{2})}}\right]+\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}<{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}}{({s^{j}}+{(m+L_{Ric}/2)}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})}}\right]+O{(\delta^{i}{\varepsilon^{1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{3/2})}

(B.7)

where we define
\alpha_{\varepsilon}:=\min\left\{\frac{m-L_{Ric}/2}{16},\frac{1}{2{(1+\varepsilon)}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}\right\}\exp{(-\frac{1}{2}(1+\varepsilon)\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}^{2})}
. The first line follows from Lemma
12
, the second line simply splits
{\mathbbm{1}\left\{r>\mathcal{R}\right\}}
into two cases:
{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathcal{R}<{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\leq\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\right\}}
and
{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}<{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}}
, and bounds
\kappa(r)\leq-m
when
r>\mathcal{R}
under Assumption
2
. The third inequality is by definition of
\alpha_{\varepsilon}
.

Applying (
B.7
) recursively for
k=0...K
, where
K=T/2^{i}
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{({(\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{K})}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha_{\varepsilon}K\delta^{i})}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(r_{0}^{2})}}\right]+O{(T\varepsilon^{1/2}+T\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{1/2})}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}<{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}}{({s^{j}}+{(m+L_{Ric}/2)}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})}}\right]+O{(T{\varepsilon^{1/2}}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}T{\delta^{i}}^{1/2})}

(B.8)

We now bound the second term of (
B.8
) more carefully:

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}}{({s^{j}}+{(m+L_{Ric}/2)}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{{(\max_{k\leq K}{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}})}\sum_{k=0}^{K}{({s^{j}}+{(m+L_{Ric}/2)}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\mathbb{E}\left[{{({\mathbbm{1}\left\{\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq\max_{k\leq K}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}\right\}})}\sum_{k=0}^{K}{({s^{j}}+{(m+L_{Ric}/2)}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta^{i}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}{({\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq\max_{k\leq K}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}})}}\cdot\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{{(\sum_{k=0}^{K}{({s^{j}}+{(m+L_{Ric}/2)}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})})}^{2}}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O{(\frac{1}{s^{j}})}

(B.9)

The last line is because of the following: from Lemma
16
,
\delta^{i}\mathbb{P}{({\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\leq\sup_{k\leq K}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}}})}^{1/2}=O{(\frac{{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}{(s^{j}-L_{0})^{2}})}=O{(\frac{1}{s_{j}^{2}})}
assuming
j
sufficiently large. Also from Lemma
16
,
\delta^{i}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{{(\sum_{k=0}^{K}{({s^{j}}+{(m+L_{Ric}/2)}{\tilde{r}^{i,j}_{k}})})}^{2}}\right]}=O{(T)}
.

Plugging (
B.9
) into (
B.8
), and recalling the definition of
\tilde{r}
, and the fact that
\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i}_{K},\tilde{y}^{i}_{K})}:=\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i}(T),\tilde{y}^{i}(T))}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T)}^{2})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha_{\varepsilon}K\delta^{i})}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x(0),y(0))}^{2})}+O{(\varepsilon^{1/2}+\varepsilon^{-5/2}{\delta^{i}}^{1/2}+\frac{1}{s^{j}})}

where
O(\cdot)
hides
T
dependency as well.

First, by taking the limit of
i
to infinity (e.g. for each
i
, we see that for any
j
and any
\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T))}^{2})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha_{\varepsilon}K\delta^{i})}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x(0),y(0))}^{2})}+O{(\varepsilon^{1/2}+\frac{1}{s^{j}})}

Let us define
\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(t)
as the almost sure limit of
\tilde{x}^{i,j}(t)
, as
i\to\infty
, whose existence is shown in Lemma
5
(similarly for
\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(t)
). It follows that
g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T)}^{2})}
converges almost surely to
g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(T))}^{2})}
as
i\to\infty
. By dominated convergence (Lemma
15
implies a single constant upper bounds
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T)}^{2}}\right]
for all
i
),
\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T))}^{2})}}\right]
converges to
\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(T))}^{2})}}\right]
as
i\to\infty
. Let
\Omega
denote the set of all couplings between
\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(t)
and
\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(t)
. Then

\displaystyle\inf_{\Omega}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(T))}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{i,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{i,j}(T))}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha_{\varepsilon}K\delta^{i})}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x(0),y(0))}^{2})}+O{(\varepsilon^{1/2}+\frac{1}{s^{j}})},

(B.10)

where the first inequality uses the fact that
\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(t)
(resp
\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(t)
) is the limit, as
i\to\infty
, of
\tilde{x}^{i,j}(t)
(resp
\tilde{y}^{i,j}(t)
).

From Lemma
16
, we know that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\sup_{t}\mathrm{d}{(x(t),x(0))}\geq s})}\leq O{(\frac{1}{s^{4}})},

where we use the fact that, by definition in (
A.3
),
x^{i}(t)
are linear interpolations of
x^{i}(k)
. Next, notice that when
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathrm{d}{(x(t),x(0))}\leq\frac{s^{j}-L_{0}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}
,
x(t)=\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
. It thus follows that as
s^{j}\to\infty
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}^{\cdot,j}(T),\tilde{y}^{\cdot,j}(T))})}}\right]
converges to
\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x(T),y(T))})}}\right]
almost surely. Thus taking limit of (
B.10
) as
j\to\infty
, i.e.
s^{j}\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\inf_{\Omega}\mathbb{E}\left[{g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x(T),y(T))}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-\alpha_{\varepsilon}K\delta^{i})}g_{\varepsilon}{(\mathrm{d}{(x(0),y(0))}^{2})}+O{(\varepsilon^{1/2})}.

Finally, take the limit of
\varepsilon\to 0
. Note that
g_{\varepsilon}(r^{2})\to g_{0}(r^{2})=f(r)
, where
f
is defined in Definition
2
. Note also that
\alpha_{\varepsilon}\to\alpha
as defined in the lemma statement. Finally, the properties of
f
follows from Lemma
10
.

∎

Appendix C
Tail Bounds

In this section, we establish various probability and moment bounds for and (
1.1
), (
1.2
) and (
2.1
). These bounds are used at many places in our proofs.

C.1
One-Step Distance Bounds

C.1.1
Under Lipschitz Continuity

Lemma 13

(One-step distance evolution under Lipschitz Continuity)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
3
. Let
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a stepsize satisfying
\delta\leq{\frac{1}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}}
. Let
x_{0}\in M
be arbitrary, let
x_{k}
denote the iterative process

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

where
\xi_{k}
denote a random variable that possibly depends on
x_{k}
. Let
\gamma_{k}(t):[0,1]\to M
denote any minimizing geodesic with
\gamma(0)=x_{k}
and
\gamma(1)=x_{0}
. Then for any positive integer
K
and for any
k\leq K
, we can bound,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+8\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2K}+\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+2\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+8K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\leq\frac{1}{\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}\right\}}{(-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{})}

Proof.

We will be using the bound from
Zhang and Sra 2016
(see Lemma
25
). Let
v:=\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})
. Then Lemma
25
bounds

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x)}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}-2\bm{\left\langle}v,\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+{\tc{(\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})})}}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}-2\bm{\left\langle}v,\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x_{0}))}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}

(C.1)

where
\zeta(r):=\frac{r}{\tanh(r)}
.

We will consider two cases:

Case 1:
\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\leq\frac{1}{\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}
.

From (
C.1
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x_{0}))}{\left\|\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+\delta^{2}{({L_{0}}^{2}+{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2})}+\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta^{2}\sqrt{L_{R}}{(L_{0}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}+{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{3})}+\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|{\xi_{k}(x_{k})}\right\|}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}+\frac{1}{4K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}+\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{,}x_{0})}^{2}+\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}+\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+{\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+3\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+\frac{1}{4K}+\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+{(2K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}+\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+{\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}})}

where the third inequality uses the definition of Case 1, and the fourth inequality is by several applications of Young’s Inequality.

Case 2:
\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}>\frac{1}{4\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}
.

Let us define

\displaystyle z(t):=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(t{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))})}

I.e.
z(t)
interpolates between
x_{k}
and
x_{k+1}
. We verify that
z^{\prime}(t)=\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}
. Let us also define a family of geodesics
\gamma_{t}
, where for each
t
,
\gamma_{t}
is a minimizing geodesic with
\gamma_{t}(0)=z(t)
and
\gamma_{t}(1)=x_{0}
. If such a minimizing geodesic is not unique, any choice will do. We verify that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{0})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle-2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0),z^{\prime}(t)\bm{}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(z(t)),\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}}_{\hbox to9.22pt{\vbox to9.22pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.60942pt\lower-4.60942pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(z(t))-\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\delta\beta(x_{k}))},\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}}_{\hbox to9.22pt{\vbox to9.22pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.60942pt\lower-4.60942pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}-\underbrace{2\bm{\left\langle}\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))},\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}}_{\hbox to9.22pt{\vbox to9.22pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.60942pt\lower-4.60942pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}.

Let’s upper bound the terms one by one.

We first bound
2
, which represents the "discretization error in drift":

\displaystyle\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
