\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}:=

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(z(t))-\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\delta\beta(x_{k}))},\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2{\left\|\delta\beta(z(t))-\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\delta\beta(x_{k}))}\right\|}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{k})}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{k})}^{2}+2\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{0})}^{2}

By definition of
z(t)
, we know that
\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{k})}\leq{\left\|\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}\leq\delta L_{0}+\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}+\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}
, so that
2\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{k})}^{2}\leq 8\delta^{3}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{3}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+8\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+8\delta^{3}L_{\beta}^{\prime}L_{0}^{2}\leq\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}
, so that

\displaystyle\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\leq

\displaystyle\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{k})}^{2}+\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}

Next, we bound
3
, which is the most significant error term. From the definition of Case 2,
\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}>\frac{1}{\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}
,

\displaystyle\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\leq

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))},\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{0})}^{2}+\frac{1}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{0})}^{2}+{\delta L_{R}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}

where we use our assumption that
\delta\leq\frac{1}{{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}
.

Finally, we bound
1
as

\displaystyle-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(z(t)),\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq

\displaystyle 4\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{0})}^{2}+4K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}+\frac{1}{4K}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{0})}^{2}

Putting everything together,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{0})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(6\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{4K})}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{0})}^{2}+{(\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+4K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}

By Gronwall’s Lemma (integrating from
t=0
to
t=1
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(z(1),x_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(6\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{4K})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+{(2\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+2\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+2\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+8K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+8\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2K}+\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+2\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+8K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}

where we use the assumption that
\delta\leq\frac{1}{8L_{\beta}^{\prime}}
.

Combining Case 1 and Case 2:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+8\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2K}+\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+2\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+8K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\leq\frac{1}{\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}\right\}}{(-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{})}

∎

C.1.2
Under Dissipativity

Lemma 14

(One-step distance evolution under Dissipativity)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
3
. Let
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a stepsize satisfying
\delta\leq{\frac{m}{128{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}}
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Let
x_{0}\in M
be arbitrary. Let
x_{k}
be the iterative process

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2},

and let
\gamma_{k}
denote such a geodesic for
x=x_{k}
. Then for any
k
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+{\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}}{m^{5}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\leq\frac{m}{4\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}\right\}}{(-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{})}

Proof.

Throughout the proof, it is useful to note that by our assumptions, it must be that
m\leq L_{\beta}^{\prime}
. We will be using the bound from
Zhang and Sra 2016
(see Lemma
25
). Let
v:=\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})
. Then Lemma
25
bounds

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x)}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}-2\bm{\left\langle}v,\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+{\tc{(\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}-2\bm{\left\langle}v,\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}

(C.2)

where
\zeta(r):=\frac{r}{\tanh(r)}
.

We will consider two cases:

Case 1:
\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\leq\frac{m}{4\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}
.

From (
C.2
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k},x^{*})}-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k},x^{*})}-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta^{2}\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{3}+\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{\left\|{\xi_{k}(x_{k})}\right\|}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+\delta m/2)}\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k},x^{*})}-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+\frac{4\delta L_{R}}{m}{\left\|{\xi_{k}(x_{k})}\right\|}^{4}

(C.3)

where we use our assumptions that
\delta\leq m/{(16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2})}
and the inequality under Case 1. We used Cauchy Schwarz a few times.

We can further bound

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}\right\}}{(-2m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2})}+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\leq\mathcal{R}\right\}}{(2L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-2\delta m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+2\delta{(m+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}\mathcal{R}^{2}

Thus

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k},x^{*})}-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+2\delta{(m+L_{\beta}^{\prime})}\mathcal{R}^{2}+\frac{4\delta L_{R}}{m}{\left\|{\xi_{k}(x_{k})}\right\|}^{4}

(C.4)

where we use the fact that
e^{\delta m}\leq e^{\frac{m^{2}}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}}\leq 2
.

Case 2:
\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}>\frac{m}{4\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}
.

Let us define

\displaystyle z(t):=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(t{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))})}

I.e.
z(t)
interpolates between
x_{k}
and
x_{k+1}
. We verify that
z^{\prime}(t)=\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}
. Let us also define a family of geodesics
\gamma_{t}
, where for each
t
,
\gamma_{t}
is a minimizing geodesic with
\gamma_{t}(0)=z(t)
and
\gamma_{t}(1)=x^{*}
. If such a minimizing geodesic is not unique, any choice will do. We also verify that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle-2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0),z^{\prime}(t)\bm{}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(z(t)),\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}}_{\hbox to9.22pt{\vbox to9.22pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.60942pt\lower-4.60942pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.1 0 C 6.1 3.37 3.37 6.1 0 6.1 C -3.37 6.1 -6.1 3.37 -6.1 0 C -6.1 -3.37 -3.37 -6.1 0 -6.1 C 3.37 -6.1 6.1 -3.37 6.1 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(z(t))-\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\delta\beta(x_{k}))},\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}}_{\hbox to9.22pt{\vbox to9.22pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.60942pt\lower-4.60942pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.1 0 C 6.1 3.37 3.37 6.1 0 6.1 C -3.37 6.1 -6.1 3.37 -6.1 0 C -6.1 -3.37 -3.37 -6.1 0 -6.1 C 3.37 -6.1 6.1 -3.37 6.1 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}-\underbrace{2\bm{\left\langle}\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))},\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}}_{\hbox to9.22pt{\vbox to9.22pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.60942pt\lower-4.60942pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.1 0 C 6.1 3.37 3.37 6.1 0 6.1 C -3.37 6.1 -6.1 3.37 -6.1 0 C -6.1 -3.37 -3.37 -6.1 0 -6.1 C 3.37 -6.1 6.1 -3.37 6.1 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

Let’s upper bound the terms one by one.

We first bound
2
, which represents the "discretization error in drift":

\displaystyle\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}:=

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(z(t))-\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\delta\beta(x_{k}))},\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2{\left\|\delta\beta(z(t))-\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\delta\beta(x_{k}))}\right\|}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{k})}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\delta m}{4}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}{m}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{k})}^{2}

By definition of
z(t)
, we know that
\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{k})}\leq{\left\|\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}\leq\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}+\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}
, so that
\frac{4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}{m}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x_{k})}^{2}\leq\frac{4\delta^{3}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}}{m}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{4\delta^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}{m}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}\leq\frac{\delta m}{8}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}
, so that

\displaystyle\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\delta m}{4}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{\delta m}{8}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

Next, we bound
3
, which is the most significant error term. From the definition of Case 2,
\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}>\frac{1}{4\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}
,

\displaystyle\hbox to11.37pt{\vbox to11.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.68657pt\lower-5.68657pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.59 0 C 7.59 4.19 4.19 7.59 0 7.59 C -4.19 7.59 -7.59 4.19 -7.59 0 C -7.59 -4.19 -4.19 -7.59 0 -7.59 C 4.19 -7.59 7.59 -4.19 7.59 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\leq

\displaystyle 2\bm{\left\langle}\Gamma_{z(0)}^{z(t)}{(\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))},\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\delta}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\delta m}{8}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{8}{m}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\delta m}{8}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{32\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}{m^{2}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\delta m}{8}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{\delta m}{8}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}}{m^{5}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}

where we use our assumption that
\delta\leq\frac{m}{128{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}
.

Finally, we bound
1
as

\displaystyle-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta\beta(z(t)),\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}^{2}\geq\mathcal{R}\right\}}{(-2\delta m\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}^{2})}+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}\leq\mathcal{R}\right\}}{(2\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathrm{d}{(z(t)x^{*})})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}^{2}-2\delta m\mathrm{d}{(\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}^{2})}

Putting everything together,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{3\delta m}{2}\mathrm{d}{(z(t),x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{\delta m}{4}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}}{m^{5}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}

By Gronwall’s Lemma (integrating from
t=0
to
t=1
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}=

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(z(1),x^{*})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(-{3\delta m/2})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{\delta m}{4}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}}{m^{5}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}}{m^{5}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}

(C.5)

where we use the assumption that
\delta\leq\frac{1}{128m}
so that
\exp{(-3\delta m/2)}\leq 1-5\delta m/4
.

Combining Case 1 and Case 2:

Combining (
C.4
) and (
C.5
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+{(\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}}{m^{5}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2})}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\leq\frac{1}{4\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}\right\}}{(-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{})}

∎

C.2
L_{p}
Bounds

C.2.1
Under Lipschitz Continuity

Lemma 15

(L2 Bound and Chevyshev under Lipschitz Continuity)
.

Consider the same setup as Lemma
13
. Assume in addition that there exists
\sigma_{\xi}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that for all
x
and for all
k
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\xi_{k}(x)\right\|}^{2}}\right]\leq\sigma_{\xi}^{2}
. Then for any positive integer
K
, and for all
k\leq K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}}\right]\leq 4\exp{(8K\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+K\delta L_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+K\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}\cdot{(2K\delta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+8K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2})}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\geq s})}\leq\frac{4}{s^{2}}\exp{(8K\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+K\delta L_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+K\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}\cdot{(2K\delta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+8K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2})}

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{k}
denote the
\sigma
-field generated by
\xi_{0}...\xi_{k-1}
.

To bound the first claim, take expectation of the bound from Lemma
13
wrt
{\mathcal{F}}_{k}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{{(1+8\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2K}+\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}}}\right]+2\delta\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}}\right]+8K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\leq\frac{1}{\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}\right\}}{(-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+8\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2K}+\delta L_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}}+2\delta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+8K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(8\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2K}+\delta L_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}}+2\delta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+8K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}

In line 3 above,
\gamma_{k}(t)
is a minimizing geodesic from
x_{k}
to
x_{0}
, as defined in Lemma
13
.

Applying the above recursively,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{K},x_{0})}^{2}}\right]\leq\exp{(1+8K\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+K\delta L_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+K\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}\cdot{(2K\delta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+8K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2})}

The above upper bound clearly also holds for
\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}}\right]
for all
k\leq K
. This proves our first claim.

To prove the second claim, let us define

\displaystyle r_{0}^{2}:=0

\displaystyle r_{k+1}^{2}:={(1+8\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{4K}+\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}r_{k}^{2}+2\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+8K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\leq\frac{1}{\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}\right\}}{(-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{})}

We verify that
r_{k}
as defined above is a sub-martingale. Thus by Doob’s martingale inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}r_{k}^{2}\geq s})}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{r_{K}^{2}}\right]}{s}

Furthermore, notice that

\displaystyle r_{0}^{2}=\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x_{0})}^{2}=0

\displaystyle r_{k+1}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1}^{2},x_{0})}^{2}\geq{(1+8\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\frac{2}{K}+\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}{(r_{k}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2})}\geq 0

so that
r_{k}\geq\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}
with probability 1, for all
k
.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\geq s})}\leq\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}r_{k}^{2}\geq s^{2}})}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{r_{K}^{2}}\right]}{s^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{s^{2}}\exp{(1+8K\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+K\delta L_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+K\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}\cdot{(2K\delta\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+8K^{2}\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2})}

The proof for the bound on
r_{K}^{2}
is identical to the proof of the first claim. We conclude our proof of the second claim
∎

Lemma 16

(L4 Bound and Chevyshev under Lipschitz Continuity)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
3
. Assume in addition that
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfies
\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}},\frac{1}{16\sqrt{L_{R}}L_{0}},\frac{1}{L_{R}d}\right\}
. Let
L_{0}:={\left\|\beta(x_{0})\right\|}
. Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

Assume in addition that for all
x
and for all
k
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\xi_{k}(x)\right\|}^{4}}\right]\leq 2d^{2}
. Then for any positive
K\geq 4
, and for all
k\leq K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{4}}\right]\leq\exp{(2+16K\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+4K\delta L_{R}d+3K\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}{(5K^{2}\delta^{2}d^{2}+64K^{4}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4})}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\geq s})}\leq\frac{1}{s^{4}}\exp{(2+16K\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+4K\delta L_{R}d+3K\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}{(5K^{2}\delta^{2}d^{2}+64K^{4}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4})}

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{k}
denote the
\sigma
-field generated by
\xi_{0}...\xi_{k-1}
.

We will use the following inequality from Lemma
13
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+8\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2K}+\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2}+2\delta{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+8K\delta^{2}L_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\leq\frac{1}{\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}}\right\}}{(-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{})}

Squaring both sides,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+16\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+3\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}{(x_{k})}\right\|}^{2}+3\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2}+2\delta^{2}L_{R}^{2}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+\frac{2}{K})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+5K\delta^{2}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+64K^{3}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox to12.21pt{\vbox to12.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.10283pt\lower-6.10283pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

(C.6)

where
*
has
0
-mean, and we used a few times Cauchy Schwarz and Young’s Inequality.

Taking expectation wrt
{\mathcal{F}}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x_{0})}^{4}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+16\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+3\delta L_{R}d+3\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2}+2\delta^{2}L_{R}^{2}d^{2}+\frac{2}{K})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{4}+5K\delta^{2}d^{2}+64K^{3}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1+16\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+4\delta L_{R}d+3\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2}+\frac{2}{K})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{4}+5K\delta^{2}d^{2}+64K^{3}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4}

Applying the above recursively,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{K},x_{0})}^{4}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(2+16K\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+4K\delta L_{R}d+3K\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}{(5K^{2}\delta^{2}d^{2}+64K^{4}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4})}

To prove the second claim, define

\displaystyle r_{0}^{4}:=0

\displaystyle r_{k+1}^{4}:={(1+16\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+3\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}{(x_{k})}\right\|}^{2}+3\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2}+2\delta^{2}L_{R}^{2}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+\frac{2}{K})}r_{k}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+5K\delta^{2}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+64K^{3}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4}

\displaystyle\quad+\hbox to12.21pt{\vbox to12.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.10283pt\lower-6.10283pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 8.17 0 C 8.17 4.51 4.51 8.17 0 8.17 C -4.51 8.17 -8.17 4.51 -8.17 0 C -8.17 -4.51 -4.51 -8.17 0 -8.17 C 4.51 -8.17 8.17 -4.51 8.17 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.5pt}{-3.75pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

where
*
is the same term as (
C.6
).

We verify that
r_{k}
as defined above is a sub-martingale. Thus by Doob’s martingale inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}r_{k}^{2}\geq s})}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{r_{K}^{2}}\right]}{s}

Furthermore, notice that

\displaystyle r_{0}^{2}=

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x_{0})}^{2}=0

\displaystyle r_{k+1}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1}^{2},x_{0})}^{2}\geq

\displaystyle{(1+16\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+3\delta L_{R}{\left\|\xi_{k}{(x_{k})}\right\|}^{2}+3\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2}+2\delta^{2}L_{R}^{2}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+\frac{2}{K})}{(r_{k}^{2}-\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}^{2})}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 0

so that
r_{k}\geq\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}
with probability 1, for all
k
.

Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x_{0})}\geq s})}\leq\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}r_{k}\geq s})}\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[{r_{K}^{4}}\right]}{s^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{s^{4}}\exp{(2+16K\delta L_{\beta}^{\prime}+4K\delta L_{R}d+3K\delta^{2}L_{R}L_{0}^{2})}{(5K^{2}\delta^{2}d^{2}+64K^{4}\delta^{4}L_{0}^{4})}

The proof for the bound on
r_{K}^{2}
is identical to the proof of the first claim. We conclude our proof of the second claim
∎

C.2.2
Under Dissipativity

Lemma 17

(L4 Bound under Dissipativity, Discretized SDE)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
3
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2}

. Assume in addition that
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
satisfies
\delta\leq{\frac{m}{128{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}}

Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

where
\xi_{k}
is a random vector field satisfying
\mathbb{E}\left[{\xi_{k}(x)}\right]=0
and
\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\xi_{k}\right\|}^{4}}\right]\leq{(\sigma_{\xi})}^{4}
.

For any
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{K},x^{*})}^{4}}\right]\leq e^{-K\delta m}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}^{4}}\right]+\frac{2^{24}L_{R}^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{8}}{m^{12}}\sigma_{\xi}^{8}+\frac{64{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{4}}{m^{2}}+\frac{128}{m^{2}}\sigma_{\xi}^{4}

Proof.

Let
\gamma_{k}
denote a geodesic with
\gamma_{k}(0)=x_{k},\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}

From Lemma
14
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+{\frac{2048\delta L_{R}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{4}}{m^{5}}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}+4\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\leq\frac{m}{4\delta\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}}\right\}}{(-2\bm{\left\langle}\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{})}

Squaring both sides and taking expectation wrt
\xi_{k}
(and applying Young’s Inequality),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{4}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\frac{3\delta m}{2})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{4}+\frac{2^{24}\delta L_{R}^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{8}}{m^{11}}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{8}}\right]+\frac{64\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{4}}{m}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\delta m}{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{4}+\frac{128\delta}{m}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(1-\delta m)}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{4}+\frac{2^{24}\delta L_{R}^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{8}}{m^{11}}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{8}}\right]+\frac{64\delta{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{4}}{m}+\frac{128\delta}{m}\mathbb{E}\left[{{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{4}}\right]

Applying the above recursively,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{K},x^{*})}^{4}}\right]\leq e^{-K\delta m}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}^{4}}\right]+\frac{2^{24}L_{R}^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{8}}{m^{12}}\sigma_{\xi}^{8}+\frac{64{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{4}}{m^{2}}+\frac{128}{m^{2}}\sigma_{\xi}^{4}

∎

Lemma 18

(L4 Bound under Dissipativity, Exact SDE)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
3
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2}

.
Let
x(t)
denote the solution to (
1.2
).
For any
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x(T),x^{*})}^{4}}\right]\leq\exp{(-K\delta m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{0},x^{*})}^{4}}\right]+\frac{2^{26}L_{R}^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{8}}{m^{12}}d^{4}+\frac{64{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{4}}{m^{2}}+\frac{256}{m^{2}}d^{2}

Proof.

For
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, let
\delta^{i}
be a sequence of stepsizes going to
0
, let
K^{i}:=T/\delta^{i}
, and let
x^{i}_{k}
be a discretization of
x(t)
with stepsize
\delta^{i}
of the form (
1.2
), i.e.

\displaystyle x^{i}_{k+1}=\Exp_{x^{i}_{k}}{(\delta^{i}\beta(x^{i}_{k})+\sqrt{\delta^{i}}\zeta_{k})}

where
\zeta_{k}\sim\mathcal{N}_{x^{i}_{k}}(0,I)
.
Applying Lemma
17
to
x^{i}_{k}
with
\xi_{k}(x_{k})=\zeta_{k}
and
\sigma_{\xi}=\sqrt{2}d
, and for
i
sufficiently large, gives the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{K^{i}},x^{*})}^{4}}\right]\leq\exp{(-K^{i}\delta^{i}m)}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathrm{d}{(x^{i}_{0},x^{*})}^{4}}\right]+\frac{2^{26}L_{R}^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{8}}{m^{12}}d^{4}+\frac{64{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{4}}{m^{2}}+\frac{256}{m^{2}}d^{2}

Our conclusion follows by Lemma
2
as
x^{i}_{K^{i}}
converges to
x(T)
almost surely as
i\to\infty
.
∎

C.3
Subgaussian Bounds

C.3.1
Under Dissipativity

Lemma 19

(Subgaussian Bound under Dissipativity, Discrete Time Semimartingale, Adaptive Stepsize)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying
3
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2}.

Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}

where
\xi_{k}
is a random vector field. Assume that for all
x
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\xi_{k}(x)}\right]=0
, and that for any
\rho\leq\frac{1}{8}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\rho{\left\|\xi_{k}(x)\right\|}^{2})}}\right]\leq\exp{(\rho\sigma_{\xi}^{2})}
. For each
k
,
\delta_{k}
is a positive stepsize that depends only on
x_{k}
and satisfies

\delta_{k}\leq\min\left\{\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}},\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{m{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}},\frac{32\sigma_{\xi}^{4}}{m^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right\}
. Assume that
\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}\leq 2\mathcal{R}
. Finally, assume that there exists
\delta\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that for all
k
,
\delta_{k}\leq\delta
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq t})}\leq

\displaystyle 32K\delta\lambda\exp{(\frac{2L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}+\frac{16L_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{m}-\frac{mt^{2}}{64\sigma_{\xi}^{2}})}

Proof.

For each
k
, let
\gamma_{k}
be a minimizing geodesic with
\gamma_{k}(0)=x_{k}
and
\gamma_{k}(1)=x^{*}
satisfying
\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}
.

Using the result from Corollary 8 of
Zhang and Sra 2016
(see Lemma
25
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}^{2}{(x_{k},x^{*})}-2\bm{\left\langle}\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}

\displaystyle\quad+{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\delta_{k}\beta(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

By Assumption
2
and Assumption
3
,
\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+2L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}^{2}
. Applying Cauchy Schwarz and simplifying,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(x_{k+1},x^{*})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(-2m\delta_{k}+2\delta_{k}^{2}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}+2\delta_{k}^{2}\sqrt{L_{R}}{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+4\delta_{k}L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+2\delta_{k}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\delta_{k}m\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+4\delta_{k}L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}^{2}+2\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+2\delta_{k}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

where we used our assumption that
\delta_{k}\leq\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})})}}
.

Let
s:=\frac{m}{32\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}
We will now apply Lemma
24
with

\displaystyle q_{k}=s\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}\qquad\nu_{k}=s\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+2\sqrt{\delta_{k}}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}

\displaystyle\lambda=m\qquad\gamma=4sL_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}^{2}\qquad\mu=2s\sigma_{\xi}^{2}+\frac{64sL_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{4}}{m}

(C.7)

We will verify Lemma
24
’s condition regarding
\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\nu_{k})}}\right]
. Taking expectation conditioned on
\xi_{0}(x_{0})...\xi_{k-1}(x_{k-1})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\nu_{k})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}s\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{}+2\delta_{k}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}2s\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{})}}\right]^{1/2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(4\delta_{k}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}}\right]^{1/2}

By our assumption on
\xi_{k}
and Lemma
22
, and our assumption that
\delta_{k}\leq\frac{32\sigma_{\xi}^{4}}{m^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}2s\bm{\left\langle}\xi_{k}(x_{k}),\gamma_{k}^{\prime}(0)\bm{})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(8\delta_{k}s^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(8\delta_{k}s^{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}\sigma_{\xi}^{2})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\frac{sm\delta_{k}}{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2})}}\right]

On the other hand, by our assumption on
\xi_{k}
and
\delta_{k}\leq\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{m{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(4\delta_{k}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(4\delta_{k}s{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}(x_{k},x^{*}))}\sigma_{\xi}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(\frac{sm\delta_{k}}{2}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{128\delta_{k}sL_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{4}}{m}+4\delta_{k}s\sigma_{\xi}^{2})}

Plugging both of the above into (
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\nu_{k})}}\right]\leq\exp{(\frac{\delta_{k}ms}{4}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}^{2}+\frac{64\delta_{k}sL_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{4}}{m}+2\delta_{k}s\sigma_{\xi}^{2})}

We thus verify that
(\nu_{k},\lambda,\mu)
satisfy the requirement for Lemma
24
, which bounds

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}q_{i}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle 8K\delta\lambda\exp{(q_{0}+\frac{8(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8K\delta\lambda\exp{(\frac{m\mathcal{R}^{2}}{16\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}+\frac{L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{16L_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{m}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16K\delta\lambda\exp{(\frac{2L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}+\frac{16L_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{m}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

where the second inequality plugs in definitions from (
C.7
), uses our assumption on
x_{0}
. Finally, using the fact that
q_{k}=s\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{i},x^{*})}\geq t})}\leq

\displaystyle 32K\delta\lambda\exp{(\frac{2L_{\beta}^{\prime}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}+\frac{16L_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{m}-\frac{mt^{2}}{64\sigma_{\xi}^{2}})}

∎

Lemma 20

(Subgaussian Bound under Dissipativity, SGLD, fixed stepsize)
.

Let
\beta
be a vector field satisfying Assumption
3
. Let
x^{*}
be some point with
\beta(x^{*})=0
. Assume that for all
x
such that
\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}\geq\mathcal{R}
, there exists a minimizing geodesic
\gamma:[0,1]\to M
with
\gamma(0)=x,\gamma(1)=x^{*}
, and

\displaystyle\bm{\left\langle}\beta(x),\gamma^{\prime}(0)\bm{}\leq-m\mathrm{d}{(x,x^{*})}^{2}

.
Let
r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
denote an arbitrary radius, and assume that
\delta
is a stepsize satisfying

\displaystyle\delta\leq\min\left\{\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}r)}},\frac{d+\sigma^{2}}{m{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}r)}},\frac{32{(d^{2}+\sigma^{4})}}{m^{2}r^{2}}\right\}

.
Let
x_{k}
be the following stochastic process:

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\tilde{\beta}_{k}(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\zeta_{k}(x_{k}))}

where
\zeta_{k}(x_{k})\sim\mathcal{N}_{x_{k}}{(0,I)}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{k}(x)
satisfies, for all
x
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\tilde{\beta}_{k}(x)}\right]=\beta(x)
and
{\left\|\tilde{\beta}_{k}(x)-\beta(x)\right\|}\leq\sigma
. Assume that
\mathrm{d}{(x_{0},x^{*})}\leq 2\mathcal{R}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq r})}\leq 32K\delta m\exp{(\frac{2{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{d+\sigma^{2}}+\frac{64L_{R}{(d+\sigma^{2})}}{m}-\frac{mr^{2}}{256{(d+\sigma^{2})}})}

Proof.

We begin by defining
\xi_{k}(x_{k}):=\zeta_{k}(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}{(\tilde{\beta}(x_{k})-\beta(x_{k}))}
. We verify that

\displaystyle x_{k+1}=\Exp_{x_{k}}{(\delta\tilde{\beta}_{k}(x_{k})+\sqrt{\delta}\xi_{k}(x_{k}))}.

We verify that
\mathbb{E}\left[{\xi_{k}}\right]=0
and that for any
\lambda\leq\frac{1}{8}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\lambda{\left\|\xi_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2})}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(2\lambda{\left\|\zeta_{k}(x_{k})\right\|}^{2}+2\lambda\sigma^{2})}}\right]\leq\exp{(4\lambda d+2\lambda\sigma^{2})}

where we use Lemma
21
. Let us define
\sigma_{\xi}:=\sqrt{4d+\sigma^{2}}
.

Next, let us define, for analysis purposes, the following process:

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{k+1}=\Exp_{\tilde{x}_{k}}{(\delta_{k}\beta(\tilde{x}_{k})+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\xi_{k}(\tilde{x}_{k}))}

initialized at
\tilde{x}_{0}=x_{0}
and where

\displaystyle\delta_{k}:=\min\left\{\delta,\frac{m}{16{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}_{k},x^{*})})}},\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{m{(1+\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}_{k},x^{*})})}},\frac{32\sigma_{\xi}^{4}}{m^{2}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}_{k},x^{*})}^{2}}\right\}

Define the event
A_{k}:=\left\{\max_{i\leq k}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}_{i},x^{*})}\leq r\right\}
. Under the event
A_{k}
,
\delta_{i}=\delta
for all
i\leq k
, and consequently,
\tilde{x}_{i}=x_{i}
for all
i\leq k
. Therefore,
A_{k}=\left\{\max_{i\leq k}\mathrm{d}{({x}_{i},x^{*})}\leq r\right\}
, and thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(x_{k},x^{*})}\geq r})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({A_{k}^{c}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\mathrm{d}{(\tilde{x}_{k},x^{*})}\geq r})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 32K\delta m\exp{(\frac{2{L_{\beta}^{\prime}}^{2}\mathcal{R}^{2}}{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}+\frac{16L_{R}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{m}-\frac{mr^{2}}{64\sigma_{\xi}^{2}})}

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
19
with
\xi_{k}
and
\sigma_{\xi}
as defined above.
∎

C.4
Misc

Lemma 21
.

For
\lambda\leq\frac{1}{4}
and
\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d\times d})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\lambda{\left\|\xi\right\|}_{2}^{2})}}\right]\leq\exp{(\lambda d+2\lambda^{2}d)}\leq\exp{(2\lambda d)}

Proof.

Consequence of
\chi^{2}
distribution being subexponential.
∎

Lemma 22

(Hoeffding’s Lemma)
.

Let
\eta_{k}
be a
0
-mean random variable. Then for all
\lambda
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda\eta)}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{{\eta}}\left[{\exp{(2\lambda^{2}\eta^{2})}}\right]

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda\eta)}}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda\eta-\mathbb{E}_{{\eta^{\prime}}}\left[{\lambda\eta^{\prime}}\right])}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda{(\eta-\eta^{\prime})})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta,\eta^{\prime},\varepsilon}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda\varepsilon{(\eta-\eta^{\prime})})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda^{2}{(\eta-\eta^{\prime})}^{2}/2)}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}}\left[{\exp{(\lambda^{2}{(\eta^{2}+{\eta^{\prime}}^{2})})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\eta}}\left[{\exp{(2\lambda^{2}\eta^{2})}}\right]

where
\varepsilon
is a Rademacher random variable.
∎

Lemma 23

(Corollary of Doob’s maximal inequality)
.

Let
K
be any positive integer. For any
k\leq K
, let
a_{k},b_{k},c_{k},d_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be arbitrary positive constants. Assume that for all
k
,
a_{k}\leq\frac{1}{4}
and
{a_{k}+c_{k}}\leq\frac{1}{4}
. Let
q_{k}
be a semi-martingale of the form

\displaystyle q_{k+1}\leq{(1+a_{k})}q_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k}

where
\eta_{k}
are random variables. Assume that for all
k
,
\eta_{k}
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k-1}}\right]\leq\exp{(c_{k}q_{k}+d_{k})}

Assume in addition that
q_{k}\geq 0
almost surely, for all
k
. Finally, assume that
\sum_{k=0}^{K}a_{k}+c_{k}\leq\frac{1}{8}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}q_{k}\geq t^{2}})}\leq\exp{(q_{0}+\sum_{k=0}^{K}(b_{k}+d_{k})-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

Proof.

Let us first define

\displaystyle r_{0}:=

\displaystyle q_{0}

\displaystyle r_{k+1}:=

\displaystyle{(1+a_{k})}r_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k}

i.e.
r_{k}
is very similar to
q_{k}
, only difference being that we replaced
\leq
by
=
.

We first verify that for all
k\leq K
,
r_{k}\geq q_{k}
. For
k=0
, by definition,
r_{0}=q_{0}
. Now assume that
r_{k}\geq q_{k}
for some
k
. Then for
k+1
,

\displaystyle r_{k+1}:=

\displaystyle{(1+a_{k})}r_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle{(1+a_{k})}q_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle q_{k+1}

We verify below that
\exp{(r_{k})}
is a sub-martingale: conditioning on
\eta_{0}...\eta_{k-1}
, and taking expectation wrt
\eta_{k}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(r_{k+1})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{({(1+a_{k})}r_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp{((1+a_{k})r_{k}+b_{k})}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\exp{((1+a_{k})r_{k}+b_{k})}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\exp{(r_{k})}

where the first inequality is by convexity of
\exp
, and
\mathbb{E}\left[{\eta_{k}}\right]=0
, and Jensen’s inequality.

Let us now define
s_{k}:=\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}{(1+a_{i}+c_{i})}^{-1}
. We can upper bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}r_{k+1})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}{((1+a_{k})r_{k}+b_{k}+\eta_{k})})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}(1+a_{k})r_{k}+s_{k+1}b_{k})}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}(1+a_{k})r_{k}+s_{k+1}b_{k})}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}(1+a_{k})r_{k}+s_{k+1}b_{k})}\cdot{(\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\eta_{k})}|\eta_{0}...\eta_{k}}\right])}^{s_{k+1}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k+1}(1+a_{k})r_{k}+s_{k+1}{(b_{k}+c_{k}q_{k}+d_{k})})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{k}r_{k})}}\right]\cdot\exp{(s_{k+1}{(b_{k}+d_{k})})}

where the second inequality is by Lemma
22
, the third inequality is by the fact that
s_{k}\leq 1
for all
k
and by Jensen’s inequality, the fourth inequality uses our assumption on
\eta_{k}
in the Lemma statement, as well as the fact that
s_{k}\leq 1
for all
k
. The last equality is by definition of
s_{k}
and because
q_{k}\leq r_{k}
. Applying this recursively gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{K}r_{K})}}\right]\leq\exp{(r_{0})}\cdot\exp{(\sum_{k=0}^{K}s_{k+1}(b_{k}+d_{k}))}\leq\exp{(r_{0}+\sum_{k=0}^{K}(b_{k}+d_{k}))}

By Doob’s maximal inequality (recall that we
e^{r_{k}}
is a sub-martingale),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}q_{k}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}r_{k}\geq t^{2}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{k\leq K}\exp{(s_{K}r_{k})}\geq\exp{(s_{K}t^{2})}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s_{K}r_{K})}}\right]\cdot\exp{(-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp{(r_{0}+\sum_{k=0}^{K}s_{k+1}(b_{k}+d_{k})-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

(C.9)

The first equality uses our assumption that
s_{K}=\prod_{k=0}^{K-1}{(1+a_{k}+c_{k})}^{-1}\geq e^{-\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}{4(a_{k}+c_{k})}}\geq e^{-\frac{1}{4}}\geq\frac{1}{2}
and the fact that
r_{K}\geq q_{k}\geq 0
.
∎

Lemma 24

(Uniform Bound)
.

Let
K
be any positive integer. For
k\leq K
, let
\delta_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, let
\lambda,\gamma,\mu\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Let
q_{k}
be a sequence of random numbers of the form

\displaystyle q_{k+1}\leq{(1-\delta_{k}\lambda)}q_{k}+\delta_{k}\gamma+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\nu_{k}

where
\nu_{k}
are random variables. Assume that
q_{k}
and
\nu_{k}
are measurable wrt some filtration
{\mathcal{F}}_{k}
. Assume that for all
k
,
\nu_{k}
satisfy

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\nu_{k})}|{\mathcal{F}}_{k}}\right]\leq\exp{(\delta_{k}\lambda q_{k}/2+\delta_{k}\mu)}

Assume that there is a constant
\delta
such that for all
k
,
\delta_{k}\leq\delta\leq\frac{1}{8\lambda}
. Assume in addition that
q_{k}\geq 0
almost surely, for all
k
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}q_{i}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle 8K\delta\lambda\exp{(q_{0}+\frac{8(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

Proof.

For any
s\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(sq_{k+1})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s{((1-\delta_{k}\lambda)q_{k}+\delta_{k}\gamma+\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\nu_{k})})}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s{((1-\delta_{k}\lambda)q_{k}+\delta_{k}\gamma)})}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\nu_{k})}}\right]}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s{((1-\delta_{k}\lambda)q_{k}+\delta_{k}\gamma)})}\cdot{(\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\nu_{k})}}\right])}^{s}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(s{((1-\delta_{k}\lambda/2)q_{k}+\delta_{k}(\gamma+\mu))})}}\right]

Applying the above recursively, for any
k
, we can bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(q_{k})}}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{((1-\delta_{k}\lambda/2)q_{k-1}+\delta_{k}(\gamma+\mu))}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle...

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}{(1-\delta_{i}\lambda/2)}q_{0}+\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\prod_{j=i}^{k-1}{(1-\delta_{j}\lambda/2)}{(\delta_{i}(\gamma+\mu))})}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(e^{-\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\delta_{i}}q_{0}+(\gamma+\mu)\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}e^{-\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{j=i}^{k-1}\delta_{j}}\delta_{i})}}\right]

(C.10)

Let us define
t_{k}:=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\delta_{i}
. By our assumption that
\delta_{i}\leq\frac{1}{4\lambda}
, we can verify that
\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}e^{\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{j=i}^{k-1}\delta_{j}}\delta_{i}\leq 2\int_{0}^{t_{k}}e^{-\frac{\lambda(t_{k}-t)}{2}}dt\leq\frac{4}{\lambda}
. Therefore, for all
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(q_{k})}}\right]\leq\exp{(e^{-\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\delta_{i}}q_{0}+\frac{4(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda})}

Let us now define
N:=\left\lceil\frac{1}{4\delta\lambda}\right\rceil\geq 1
(inequality is because
\delta\leq\delta_{k}\leq\frac{1}{8\lambda}
). We verify that
q_{k+1}\leq q_{k-1}+\delta_{k}(\gamma+\mu)+\eta_{k}
. Let us now apply Lemma
23
with
\eta_{k}=\sqrt{\delta_{k}}\nu_{k}
,
a_{k}=0,b_{k}=\delta_{k}\gamma,c_{k}=\lambda/2,d_{k}=\mu
and the fact that
\delta_{k}\lambda\leq 1/4
to bound, for any
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq N}q_{k+i}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\exp{(q_{k}+(\gamma+\mu)\sum_{i=0}^{N}\delta_{i+N}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}}\right]\leq\exp{(q_{0}+\frac{8(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

where we use the fact that
N\leq\frac{1}{2\delta\lambda}

Applying union bound over the events
\left\{\max_{i\leq N}q_{k+i}\geq t^{2}\right\}
for
k=0,N,2N...
, we can bound, for any positive integer
M
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq MN}q_{i}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq N}q_{jN+i}\geq t^{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{M-1}\exp{(e^{-\frac{\lambda}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{jN}\delta_{i}}q_{0}+\frac{8(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M\exp{(q_{0}+\frac{8(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

Plugging in
M=\frac{K}{N}\leq 8K\delta\lambda
, it follows that for any
K
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}{({\max_{i\leq K}q_{i}\geq t^{2}})}\leq

\displaystyle 8K\delta\lambda\exp{(q_{0}+\frac{8(\gamma+\mu)}{\lambda}-\frac{t^{2}}{2})}

∎

Lemma 25
.

Let
M
satisfy Assumption
4
. For any 3 points
x,y,z\in M
, let
u,v\in T_{y}M
be such that
z=\Exp_{y}(v)
and
x=\Exp_{y}(u)
. Assume in addition that
{\left\|u\right\|}=\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}
(i.e.
t\to\Exp_{x}(tu)
is a minimizing geodesic). Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(z,x)}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(y,x)}^{2}-2\bm{\left\langle}v,u\bm{}+{\tc{(\sqrt{L_{R}}\mathrm{d}{(y,x)})}}{\left\|v\right\|}^{2}

where
\zeta(r):=\frac{r}{\tanh(r)}
.

The above lemma is a restatement of Corollary 8 from
Zhang and Sra 2016
. Although
Zhang and Sra 2016
required minimizizng geodesics to be unique, their proof, based on Lemma 6 of the same paper, works even if minimizing geodesics are not unique.

Appendix D
Fundamental Manifold Results

In this section, we provide Taylor expansion style inequalities for the evolution of geodesics on manifold. By making use of our bounds for matrix ODEs in Section
E
, we can bound the distance between two points along geodesics under various conditions. Most of our analysis is based on some variant of the Jacobi equation
D_{t}^{2}J+R(J,\gamma^{\prime})\gamma^{\prime}=0
.

Notably,

1.

Lemma
28
quantifies the distance evolution between
x(t)=\Exp_{x}(tu)
and
y(t)=\Exp_{y}(tv)
. This is the key to proving Lemma
7
, which bounds the distance between Euler Murayama discretization (
1.2
) and (
1.1
).

2.

Lemma
29
is a more refined version of Lemma
28
. Lemma
29
is key to proving Lemma
8
, which is in turn key to proving Theorem
2
. Lemma
29
is also used to analyze the distance evolution of two processes under the Kendall-Cranston coupling in Lemma
3
.

D.1
Jacobi Field Approximations

In the following lemma, we consider a variation field
\Lambda(s,t)
, where for each
s
,
\Lambda(s,t)
is a geodesic. We bound the error between
\Lambda(s,t)
, and its Taylor approximation of various orders. This lemma is key to proving Lemma
28
and Lemma
28
.

Lemma 26
.

Let
\Lambda(s,t):[0,1]\times[0,1]\to M
be a variation field, where for each fixed
s
,
t\to\Lambda(s,t)
is a geodesic. Let us define
{\mathcal{C}}:=\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}}
. Then for all
s,t\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}\leq\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}{(\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)+tD_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0))}\right\|}\leq{(\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}-1)}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+{(\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}-1)}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}{(\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0))}\right\|}\leq{(\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}-1)}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}\leq{{\mathcal{C}}}\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}{(D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0))}\right\|}\leq{{\mathcal{C}}}\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+{(\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}-1)}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

If in addition, the derivative of the Riemannian curvature tensor is globally bounded by
L_{R}^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}{(D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0))}-t\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}{(D_{t}D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0))}\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{3}+{\mathcal{C}}^{4})}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+{(L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{3}+{\mathcal{C}}^{2})}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

Proof.

For any fixed
s
, let
E_{i}(s,0)
be a basis of
T_{\Lambda(s,0)}M
.

Let
E_{i}(s,t)
denote an orthonormal frame along
\gamma_{s}(t):=\Lambda(s,t)
, by parallel transporting
E_{i}(s,0)
.

Let
\mathbf{J}(s,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
denote the coordinates of
\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)
wrt
E_{i}(s,t)
. Let
\mathbf{K}(s,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
denote the coordinates of
D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)
wrt
E_{i}(s,t)
. Let
\mathbf{a}(s,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
denote the coordinates of
\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t)
wrt
E_{i}(s,t)
(this is constant for fixed
s
, for all
t
). Let
\mathbf{R}(s,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{4d}
be such that
\mathbf{R}^{i}_{jkl}=\bm{\left\langle}R(E_{j}(s,t),E_{k}(s,t))E_{l}(s,t),E_{i}(s,t)\bm{}
. Let
\mathbf{M}(s,t)
denote the matrix with

\mathbf{M}_{i,j}(s,t):=-\sum_{k,l}\mathbf{R}^{i}_{jkl}(s,t)\mathbf{a}_{k}(s,t)\mathbf{a}_{l}(s,t)
.

Notice that
\mathbf{M}_{i,j}
is symmetric, since
\mathbf{M}_{i,j}=\bm{\left\langle}R(E_{j},\mathbf{a})\mathbf{a},E_{i}\bm{}=\bm{\left\langle}R(\mathbf{a},E_{i})E_{j},\mathbf{a}\bm{}=\bm{\left\langle}R(E_{i},\mathbf{a})\mathbf{a},E_{j}\bm{}
, where the first equality is by interchange symmetry and second equality is by skew symmetry of the Riemannian curvature tensor. Therefore, by definition of
L_{R}
in Assumption
4
, it follows that
{\left\|\mathbf{M}(s,t)\right\|}_{2}\leq L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}^{2}=L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}
.

The Jacobi Equation states that
D_{t}D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)=-R(\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t))\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,t)
. We verify that
-\bm{\left\langle}R(\partial_{s},\partial_{t})\partial_{t},E_{i}\bm{}=-\sum_{j,k,l}\mathbf{R}^{i}_{jkl}\mathbf{J}_{j}\mathbf{a}_{k}\mathbf{a}_{l}={\left[\mathbf{M}(s,t)\mathbf{J}(s,t)\right]}_{i}
, thus
D_{t}D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}{\left[\mathbf{M}(s,t)\mathbf{J}(s,t)\right]}_{i}\cdot E_{i}(s,t)
.

We verify that
\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{J}_{i}(s,t)=D_{t}\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),E_{i}(s,t)\bm{}=\bm{\left\langle}D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),E_{i}(s,t)\bm{}=\mathbf{K}_{i}(s,t)
. We also verify that
\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{K}_{i}(s,t)=D_{t}\bm{\left\langle}D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),E_{i}(s,t)\bm{}=\bm{\left\langle}D_{t}D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),E_{i}(s,t)\bm{}={\left[\mathbf{M}(s,t)\mathbf{J}(s,t)\right]}_{i}
.

Let us now consider a fixed
s
. To simplify notation, we drop the
s
dependence. The Jacobi Equation, in coordinate form, corresponds to the following second-order ODE:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{J}(t)=\mathbf{K}(t)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathbf{K}(t)=\mathbf{M}(t)\mathbf{J}(t)dt

Define
L_{\mathbf{M}}:=L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}={\mathcal{C}}^{2}
. We verify that
L_{\mathbf{M}}\geq\max_{t\in[0,1]}{\left\|\mathbf{M}(t)\right\|}_{2}
. Then from
34
, we see that

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{J}(t)\\
\mathbf{K}(t)\end{bmatrix}=\emat{(t;\mathbf{M})}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{J}(0)\\
\mathbf{K}(0)\end{bmatrix}

From Lemma
35
,

\displaystyle\emat{(t;\begin{bmatrix}0&I\\
\mathbf{M}(t)&0\end{bmatrix})}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{A}(t)&\mathbf{B}(t)\\
\mathbf{C}(t)&\mathbf{D}(t)\end{bmatrix}

where each block is
\mathbb{R}^{2d}
, and can be bounded as

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{A}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}}t)}\leq\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{B}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}}t)}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}\leq\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{C}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq{{\mathcal{C}}}\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}}t)}\leq{{\mathcal{C}}}\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{D}(t)\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}}t)}\leq\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{A}(t)-I\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}}t)}-1\leq\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}-1

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{B}(t)-tI\right\|}_{2}\leq\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}}t)}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}-t\leq\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}-1

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{D}(t)-I\right\|}_{2}\leq\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}}t)}-1\leq\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}-1

where we use the fact that
\cosh(r)
,
\sinh(r)
and
\frac{\sinh(r)}{r}
are monotonically increasing and that
\frac{\sinh(r)}{r}-1\geq 0
for positive
r
.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}(t)=

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}(t)\mathbf{J}(0)+\mathbf{B}(t)\mathbf{K}(0)

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}(t)=

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(t)\mathbf{J}(0)+\mathbf{D}(t)\mathbf{K}(0)

(D.1)

Thus

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{J}(t)\right\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{A}(t)\mathbf{J}(0)+\mathbf{B}(t)\mathbf{K}(0)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

and

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\left\{\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)+tD_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,t)}\right\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{J}(t)-\mathbf{J}(0)-t\mathbf{K}(0)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|{(\mathbf{A}(t)-I)}\mathbf{J}(0)+{(\mathbf{B}(t)-tI)}\mathbf{K}(0)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}-1)}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+{(\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}-1)}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

and

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\left\{\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,t)}\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left\|{(\mathbf{A}(t)-I)}\mathbf{J}(0)+\mathbf{B}(t)\mathbf{K}(0)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}-1)}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

Similarly,

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{K}(t)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{C}(t)\mathbf{J}(0)\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\mathbf{D}(t)\mathbf{K}(0)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{C}}}\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

and

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\left\{D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,t)}\right\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{K}(t)-\mathbf{K}(0)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{C}(t)\mathbf{J}(0)+{(\mathbf{D}(t)-I)}\mathbf{K}(0)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{C}}}\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+{(\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}-1)}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

To prove the last bound, let us define
L_{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}:=L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{3}
. We verify that

L_{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}\geq\max_{t\in[0,1]}{\left\|\mathbf{M}(t)-\mathbf{M}(0)\right\|}_{2}
.

we know that

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\left\{D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,t)}-t\left\{D_{t}D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,t)}\right\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\mathbf{K}(t)-\mathbf{K}(0)-t\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{J}(0)\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{M}(r)\mathbf{J}(r)-\mathbf{M}(0)\mathbf{J}(0)dr\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}{\left\|\mathbf{M}(r)-\mathbf{M}(0)\right\|}_{2}{\left\|\mathbf{J}(0)\right\|}_{2}dr+\int_{0}^{t}{\left\|\mathbf{M}(r)\right\|}_{2}{\left\|\mathbf{J}(r)-\mathbf{J}(0)\right\|}_{2}dr

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}L_{\mathbf{M}}^{\prime}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,r)\right\|}+L_{\mathbf{M}}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,r)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,r)}{(\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0))}\right\|}dr

where the last line follows from (
D.1
). From our earlier results in this lemma,

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,r)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}{(\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0))}\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq{(\cosh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}-1)}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+\frac{\sinh{({{\mathcal{C}}})}}{{{\mathcal{C}}}}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq{{\mathcal{C}}}^{2}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

where the simplifications are from Lemma
39
. Put together,

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\left\{D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,t)}-t\left\{D_{t}D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,t)}\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{3}+{\mathcal{C}}^{4})}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+{(L_{R}^{\prime}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{3}+{\mathcal{C}}^{2})}e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

∎

Lemma 27
.

Let
x,y,z\in M
, with
x=\Exp_{z}(u)
,
y=\Exp_{z}(v)
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}(x,y)\leq\frac{\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}t)}}{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}}{\left\|v-u\right\|}

Proof.

Let us define the variational field

\displaystyle\Lambda(s,t)=\Exp_{z}{(t{(u+s(v-u))})}

We verify that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle u+s(v-u)

\displaystyle D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle v-u

Lemma
26
then immediately gives

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}\leq\frac{\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}t)}}{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}}{\left\|v-u\right\|}

∎

D.2
Discrete Coupling Bounds

This section presents two key lemmas which play an important role in many of our proofs. Lemma
28
analyzes the distance between
\Exp_{x}(u)
and
\Exp_{y}(v)
, as a function of
x,y
and
u,v
. Notably, Lemma
28
implies that when
u,v
are "parallel", i.e.
u-\Gamma_{y}^{x}v=0
, then the distance between
\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u),\Exp_{y}(v))}
is not much larger than
\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}
. The proof of Lemma
28
is based on a first-order expansion of the Jacobi equation for Riemannian manifolds.

The second key lemma is Lemma
29
. It considers a similar problem setup as Lemma
28
, but is based on a second-order expansion of the Jacobi equation. It thus requires an additional bound on the derivative of the Riemannian curvature tensor. The more refined distance bound in Lemma
29
is required to properly analyze the convergence of both continuous-time SDEs (Lemma
3
) as well as discrete-time stochastic processes (Lemma
8
). The term
\int_{0}^{1}\bm{\left\langle}R...\gamma^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds
in the upper bound of Lemma
29
gives rise to the Ricci curvature (as opposed to sectional curvature) dependencies in our results.

Lemma 28
.

Let
x,y\in M
. Let
\gamma(s):[0,1]\to M
be a minimizing geodesic between
x
and
y
with
\gamma(0)=x
and
\gamma(1)=y
. Let
u\in T_{x}M
and
v\in T_{y}M
. Let
u(s)
and
v(s)
be the parallel transport of
u
and
v
along
\gamma
, with
u(0)=u
and
v(1)=v
.

Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}{(\Exp_{x}(u),\Exp_{y}(v))}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle{(1+4{\mathcal{C}}^{2}e^{4{\mathcal{C}}})}\mathrm{d}{(x,y)}^{2}+32e^{{\mathcal{C}}}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}+2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}

where
{\mathcal{C}}:=\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}
.

Proof.

Let us consider the length function
E(\gamma)=\int_{0}^{1}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(s)\right\|}^{2}ds
. We define a variation of geodesics
\Lambda(s,t)
:

\displaystyle\Lambda(s,t):=\Exp_{\gamma(s)}{(t{(u(s)+s(v(s)-u(s)))})}

We verify that

\displaystyle\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle\gamma^{\prime}(s)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle u(s)+s(v(s)-u(s))

\displaystyle D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=

\displaystyle v(s)-u(s)

Consider a fixed
t
, and let
\gamma_{t}(s):=\Lambda(s,t)
(so
\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s)
is the velocity wrt
s
).

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}E(\gamma_{t})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\int_{0}^{1}{\left\|\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s)\right\|}^{2}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s),D_{t}\gamma_{t}^{\prime}(s)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t),D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0),D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0),\left\{D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,0)}-D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\bm{}ds

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\left\{\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,t)},D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\bm{}ds

(D.2)

For any
s
, and for
t=0
,
\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=\gamma^{\prime}(s)
and
D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)=v(s)-u(s)
. Using the fact that norms and inner products are preserved under parallel transport, the first term can be simplified as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0),D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\bm{}ds=2\bm{\left\langle}\gamma^{\prime}(0),v(0)-u(0)\bm{}

To bound the second and third term, we use Lemma
26
:

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}=

\displaystyle{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}

and

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}{(D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0))}\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+{(\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}-1)}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

where we use the fact that
\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}}\leq{\mathcal{C}}
.

We can thus bound the second term of (
D.2
) as

\displaystyle{\left|\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0),\left\{D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,0)}-D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\bm{}ds\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}\cdot{({\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}^{2}{({\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}+{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}^{2})}+4{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}^{2}

Finally, to bound the third term of (
D.2
), we again apply Lemma
26
:

\displaystyle{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\Gamma_{\Lambda(s,0)}^{\Lambda(s,t)}{(\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0))}\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}-1)}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+{(\frac{\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}}{\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}}})}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{(\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u(s)\right\|}+{\left\|v(s)\right\|})})}-1)}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(s)\right\|}+{(\frac{\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u(s)\right\|}+{\left\|v(s)\right\|})})}}{\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u(s)\right\|}+{\left\|v(s)\right\|})}})}{\left\|v(s)-u(s)\right\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+{(\frac{\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}}{{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

and

\displaystyle{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}{\left\|\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}+\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{R}{\left\|\partial_{t}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}^{2}})}{\left\|D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})}\sinh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})})}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+\cosh{(\sqrt{L_{R}}{({\left\|u\right\|}+{\left\|v\right\|})})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|}

for
0\leq t\leq 1
, where we usse the fact that
\cosh(r)
and
\frac{\sinh(r)}{r}
are monotonically increasing in
r
.
Put together, the third term of (
D.2
) is bounded as

\displaystyle{\left|\int_{0}^{1}2\bm{\left\langle}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)-\left\{\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,0)\right\}^{\to\Lambda(s,t)},D_{t}\partial_{s}\Lambda(s,t)\bm{}ds\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2{({(\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}-1)}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+{(\frac{\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}}{{\mathcal{C}}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|})}\cdot{({\mathcal{C}}\sinh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|\gamma^{\prime}(0)\right\|}+\cosh{({\mathcal{C}})}{\left\|v(0)-u(0)\right\|})}

\displaystyle\leq
